Hello. Well, I came in because... I've been having a bit of a hard time at work lately. I teach at a secondary school, and I just can't seem to shake this feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements are actually coded messages about me. 

I know how it sounds, and my colleagues have gently shown me that those emails are sent to everyone on the staff list. I see that they are, but... I just can't stop feeling like there's a hidden message in them directed at me specifically. It's been very distracting, so I thought I should talk to someone.

Both the worries about the coded messages and the voice I sometimes answer—they've been going on for about that long.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to manage your daily life outside of work due to these feelings and the voice you're hearing?\n\nPatient: Well, it has been getting harder, to be honest. I'm still teaching, but... I've had to ask some of my coworkers to step in and cover parts of my project lessons. And getting through the planning meetings takes a lot more effort—I usually need their help just to stay on track. \n\nOutside of work, it's definitely affecting things. I used to be very involved, but lately, I've just stopped being reliable. I haven't been showing up for my book club or going to the theater like I usually do, and I've pretty much dropped out of the neighborhood events. It's just... too much to juggle right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feel like your thoughts are disjointed or hard to follow?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, my thoughts seem clear to me. I haven't noticed any trouble putting sentences together or anything like that. I feel like I'm still able to explain what I'm thinking just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior that seem unusual or out of character for you?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I have. Um, during my lessons or when we have our art-fair meetings, I'll catch myself just stopping right in the middle of a task. I'll start rearranging markers or papers on the desk in a very particular pattern. Or sometimes... sometimes I'll just repeat a phrase from someone else instead of responding to them normally. I really feel embarrassed when it happens, because it just interrupts what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. quiet of my house or after the classroom empties, I hear a woman’s voice say my name or make a short comment about what I’m doing, clear enough that I answer before realizing no one is there.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: During lessons or art-fair meetings, I’ll catch myself stopping mid-task to rearrange markers or papers in a very particular pattern, or I’ll repeat a phrase from someone else instead I'd say it's been about four months. Yes, roughly four months. I'll start rearranging markers or papers on the desk in a very particular pattern. Or sometimes... sometimes I'll just repeat a phrase from someone else instead of responding to them normally. I really feel embarrassed when it happens, because it just interrupts what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed doing things in a very orderly or methodical way even when it's not necessary?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Aside from what I just mentioned about the markers on my desk, I haven't really noticed myself being overly methodical in general. I mean, I have to be somewhat organized for teaching, but I don't think I do things in a rigid or orderly way when it isn't needed. It really just seems to be those specific moments at work that I told you about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other beliefs that you think might not be true, but you believe them anyway, aside from the coded messages in the emails and announcements?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really had anything else like that. It's just the emails and the announcements that I've been noticing. I can't really think of any other beliefs that would fit what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are racing or difficult to control when you're trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts don't feel like they're racing or speeding up when I'm talking to people. Sometimes I might lose my train of thought or get distracted, like I mentioned before, but it doesn't feel like my mind is going too fast or anything like that. I can usually explain things just fine, unless one of those weird interruptions happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say they feel disconnected. I mean, they follow a logical path, mostly. Like I said, I sometimes lose my train of thought, but it's not like my thoughts don't make sense together or jump around randomly. They usually connect just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overwhelmingly anxious, sad, or emotionally upset due to these experiences?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... it's a little strange, obviously, dealing with the emails and the announcements, but I wouldn't say I feel overwhelmingly anxious or sad about it. I'm just trying to make sense of it, really. It hasn't made me emotionally upset or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decline in your overall functioning or ability to perform daily tasks since these experiences started?\n\nPatient: Well, I am still teaching, so I'm getting by. But... I suppose it has affected some things. I've actually needed my coworkers to step in and cover parts of my project lessons recently, and they've had to help me get through our planning meetings at school. \n\nAnd outside of work, I've just stopped being reliable. I haven't been making it to my book club, or the theater outings, and I've been missing the neighborhood events. So, yes, it's definitely made keeping up with my normal routine a bit harder.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the coded messages you think you're finding in the emails and announcements are intended specifically for you, or could they be about someone else?\n\nPatient: I believe they are specifically for me. I know my colleagues have tried to gently point out that the emails are just standard staff memos sent to everyone, or that the morning announcements are just general school news. But I just can't shake the feeling that they are actually coded messages about me. It feels very personal, like there's a hidden meaning directed right at me, even if it looks like it's for the whole school.\n\nDoctor: Have you developed a detailed explanation or theory about the coded messages in the emails and announcements?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really... put together a whole theory about it. I just know what I'm seeing and feeling when I read them. But as for a detailed explanation of why it's happening or what the end goal is, I don't have one. I haven't figured all that out yet.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello. Well, I came in because... I've been having a bit of a hard time at work lately. I'd say it's been about four months. Yes, roughly four months. I mean, my thoughts seem clear to me. I haven't noticed any trouble putting sentences together or anything like that. I feel like I'm still able to explain what I'm thinking just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior that seem unusual or out of character for you?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I have. Um, during my lessons or when we have our art-fair meetings, I'll catch myself just stopping right in the middle of a task. I'll start rearranging markers or papers on the desk in a very particular pattern. Or sometimes... sometimes I'll just repeat a phrase from someone else instead of responding to them normally. I really feel embarrassed when it happens, because it just interrupts what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed doing things in a very orderly or methodical way even when it's not necessary?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Aside from what I just mentioned about the markers on my desk, I haven't really noticed myself being overly methodical in general. I mean, I have to be somewhat organized for teaching, but I don't think I do things in a rigid or orderly way when it isn't needed. It really just seems to be those specific moments at work that I told you about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other beliefs that you think might not be true, but you believe them anyway, aside from the coded messages in the emails and announcements?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really had anything else like that. It's just the emails and the announcements that I've been noticing. I can't really think of any other beliefs that would fit what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are racing or difficult to control when you're trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts don't feel like they're racing or speeding up when I'm talking to people. Sometimes I might lose my train of thought or get distracted, like I mentioned before, but it doesn't feel like my mind is going too fast or anything like that. I'd say it's been about four months. Yes, roughly four months. I'll start rearranging markers or papers on the desk in a very particular pattern. Or sometimes... sometimes I'll just repeat a phrase from someone else instead of responding to them normally. I really feel embarrassed when it happens, because it just interrupts what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed doing things in a very orderly or methodical way even when it's not necessary?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Aside from what I just mentioned about the markers on my desk, I haven't really noticed myself being overly methodical in general. I mean, I have to be somewhat organized for teaching, but I don't think I do things in a rigid or orderly way when it isn't needed. It really just seems to be those specific moments at work that I told you about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other beliefs that you think might not be true, but you believe them anyway, aside from the coded messages in the emails and announcements?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really had anything else like that. It's just the emails and the announcements that I've been noticing. I can't really think of any other beliefs that would fit what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are racing or difficult to control when you're trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts don't feel like they're racing or speeding up when I'm talking to people. I'd say it's been about four months. Yes, roughly four months. I mean, I have to be somewhat organized for teaching, but I don't think I do things in a rigid or orderly way when it isn't needed. It really just seems to be those specific moments at work that I told you about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other beliefs that you think might not be true, but you believe them anyway, aside from the coded messages in the emails and announcements?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really had anything else like that. It's just the emails and the announcements that I've been noticing. I can't really think of any other beliefs that would fit what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are racing or difficult to control when you're trying to explain something to someone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts don't feel like they're racing or speeding up when I'm talking to people. Sometimes I might lose my train of thought or get distracted, like I mentioned before, but it doesn't feel like my mind is going too fast or anything like that. I can usually explain things just fine, unless one of those weird interruptions happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say they feel disconnected. I mean, they follow a logical path, mostly. Like I said, I sometimes lose my train of thought, but it's not like my thoughts don't make sense together or jump around randomly. They usually connect just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overwhelmingly anxious, sad, or emotionally upset due to these experiences?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... it's a little strange, obviously, dealing with the emails and the announcements, but I wouldn't say I feel overwhelmingly anxious or sad about it. I'm just trying to make sense of it, really. It hasn't made me emotionally upset or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decline in your overall functioning or ability to perform daily tasks since these experiences started?\n\nPatient: Well, I am still teaching, so I'm getting by. But... I suppose it has affected some things. I've actually needed my coworkers to step in and cover parts of my project lessons recently, and they've had to help me get through our planning meetings at school. \n\nAnd outside of work, I've just stopped being reliable. I haven't been making it to my book club, or the theater outings, and I've been missing the neighborhood events. So, yes, it's definitely made keeping up with my normal routine a bit harder.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the coded messages you think you're finding in the emails and announcements are intended specifically for you, or could they be about someone else?\n\nPatient: I believe they are specifically for me. I know my colleagues have tried to gently point out that the emails are just standard staff memos sent to everyone, or that the morning announcements are just general school news. But I just can't shake the feeling that they are actually coded messages about me. It feels very personal, like there's a hidden meaning directed right at me, even if it looks like it's for the whole school.\n\nDoctor: Have you developed a detailed explanation or theory about the coded messages in the emails and announcements?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really... put together a whole theory about it. I just know what I'm seeing and feeling when I read them. But as for a detailed explanation of why it's happening or what the end goal is, I don't have one. I haven't figured all that out yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe the emails and announcements contain coded messages about you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Honestly, that's exactly why I came in. I just can't shake the feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements at school are really coded messages about me. Even in the exact moments when my colleagues gently show me the computer screen to prove they were sent to everyone... I still believe it. The feeling just doesn't go away.\n\nDoctor: Have you developed a system or set of rules to understand or decipher the coded messages you believe are in the emails and announcements?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have a specific system or... a set of rules to decode them. Like I said, that's really the whole reason I came in. I don't have a cipher, I just... I can’t shake the feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements at school are really coded messages about me. It's just an overwhelming sense when I hear or read them. Even when my colleagues gently show me the email on their screens to prove they were sent to everyone, I still just know they're about me. I don't need a rulebook to see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you doubt that the emails and announcements actually contain coded messages about you?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't. Like I mentioned just a minute ago, that's exactly why I came in for help. I just... I never really have moments where I doubt it. The feeling just doesn't go away.\n\nDoctor: Have you developed a system or set of rules to understand or decipher the coded messages you believe are in the emails and announcements?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have a specific system or... a set of rules to decode them. Like I said, that's really the whole reason I came in. I don't have a cipher, I just... My colleagues will gently show me their screens to prove an email was sent out to everyone, and I see what they're showing me, but... it doesn't change my mind. The feeling is just always there, no matter what.\n\nDoctor: Have you developed a system or set of rules to understand or decipher the coded messages over the four months you've been experiencing them?\n\nPatient: No, like I mentioned just a moment ago, I don't use any kind of system or rules. It feels very real to me, even when my colleagues gently show me on their screens that the emails were sent to everyone.
